This perspective adds structural rigor to the framework, allowing border-agnostic causal graph rewriting to be reformulated as a rewriting system rather than a dynamical system. This shift represents a crucial step toward facilitating the treatment of reversibility.


<div id='cs.NI'></div>

# cs.NI [[Back]](#toc)

### [289] [ScaleRAN: A Runtime for Elastic GPU Resource Management in AI-RAN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23072)
*Arthur Hamard, Jérémie Leguay, Gopalasingham Aravinthan, Alberto Conte, Julien Lallet, Abdelkader Outtagarts*

Main category: cs.NI

Abstract: AI-RAN is driving the convergence of radio access network (RAN) processing and artificial intelligence (AI) workloads on GPU-enabled computing platforms, ranging from GPU-accelerated base stations to cloud-native infrastructures. While GPU acceleration improves the efficiency of virtualized RAN functions, the computational demand of latency-critical baseband processing varies with traffic load, creating transient spare GPU capacity that can be reclaimed by co-located AI applications. Existing GPU-sharing mechanisms rely on coarse-grained hardware partitioning or static resource allocation, limiting their ability to adapt to slot-level workload fluctuations while preserving strict timing requirements. This paper presents ScaleRAN, a node-local runtime for elastic GPU resource management in AI-RAN. ScaleRAN leverages NVIDIA Green Contexts to dynamically partition Streaming Multiprocessors (SMs) between RAN and AI workloads through a ladder of pre-created GPU partitions, enabling low-overhead resource reallocation at RAN slot granularity. The proposed runtime naturally supports Open-RAN (O-RAN) O-Cloud deployments, one representative AI-RAN deployment scenario. To drive these reallocations, we propose a reactive controller inspired by Kubernetes autoscaling and a proactive controller based on quantile regression and conformal prediction. We also develop LDPCSim, a GPU-accelerated workload generator built on NVIDIA Aerial for reproducible evaluation. Results show that predictive fine-grained GPU partitioning satisfies slot-level RAN deadlines while increasing the GPU resources available to co-located AI workloads, demonstrating the practicality of elastic GPU sharing for future AI-RAN platforms.


### [290] [Preventing Data Hallucinations in Agentic AI for Optical Network Troubleshooting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23252)
*Jeremias Dötterl, Martin Kuipers*

Main category: cs.NI

Abstract: An agentic AI system for automated troubleshooting in optical communication networks is demonstrated that integrates data references into the agent architecture to prevent data hallucinations, improving the interpretability and trustworthiness of root-cause identification and reasoning.


<div id='cs.GT'></div>

# cs.GT [[Back]](#toc)

### [291] [Cooperation Without Adaptation: LLM Agents in a Collective-Risk Dilemma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22882)
*Trung-Kiet Huynh, Dao-Sy Duy-Minh, Chi-Nguyen Tran, Phu-Hoa Pham, Phu-Quy Nguyen-Lam, Le Hong Trang, Anh Han*

Main category: cs.GT

Abstract: Language-model agents are increasingly deployed to act on behalf of people in settings where individual decisions shape shared outcomes. We examine whether five production models adapt their contributions to changing risks and partner behaviour, or instead follow a relatively fixed strategy. In a collective-risk dilemma game (CRG), six players contribute to a shared pool over ten rounds; if the pool falls short of its target, a catastrophe may destroy their remaining resources. Four models contributed approximately an equal share at every positive risk level, and four of the five also contributed when risk was zero, even though contributing could not improve their payoff. Explicitly stating that only the agent's own payoff mattered reduced zero-risk contributions for most models, while a more neutral prompt did not. Several models correctly identified which action offered the higher expected payoff at different risk levels, yet their contributions changed little. Against fixed partners, no model consistently chose the payoff-maximising response, and in mixed-model groups, a single agent's shortfall in the final round could cause an otherwise successful group to fail. Payoff-based selection also depended on population composition: in a single six-agent group, it favoured the model that contributed least and reduced group success, whereas in larger, randomly mixed populations, it favoured an exact fair-share model at moderate and high risk. These findings show that cooperative self-play alone is not evidence of strategic adaptability. Consistent with a machine-behaviour approach, evaluations of agents in multi-agent systems should test how they respond to incentives, partner actions, and population composition.


### [292] [How language model agents reshape price competition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21711)
*Martin Bichler, Philip Hierhager, Kassian Köck, Fabian Pieroth*

Main category: cs.GT

Abstract: Repeated Bertrand competition is a stringent benchmark for algorithmic pricing: in homogeneous-goods markets, standard learning procedures typically select the stage-game Nash outcome with prices near marginal cost. We show that LLM pricing agents can escape this benchmark, depending on deployment decisions. LLMs outperform standard online learners through faster adaptation, but this advantage largely disappears against full-information learners. An extracted surrogate algorithm illustrates how LLMs in specific deployments profit from regularly undercutting slower learners. Agents that post one price every period from raw market histories that include rival prices can sustain supracompetitive regimes in self-play. Agents that periodically emit probability distributions from summarized histories of their own profits instead move toward the competitive region in self-play. Markets mixing these interfaces can generate dynamic regimes in which LLMs establish a high-price anchor and regularly undercut it to capture demand. These regimes can keep both competitors' prices above marginal cost. Deployment choices shape these outcomes, shifting the focus from stationary equilibria to dynamic pricing regimes.


### [293] [Anytime Contracts for AI Services](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22781)
*Boris Velasevic, Eden Saig, Eric Mazumdar*

Main category: cs.GT

Abstract: AI services are prone to moral hazard, as model providers can silently route requests to cheaper, lower-quality models, and the inherent stochasticity of model outputs makes this degradation hard to detect. Contract theory addresses moral hazard by designing payment rules that reward high-quality outputs and prevent strategic overcharging. However, existing frameworks assume interactions of fixed duration, whereas AI services are typically consumed through subscriptions and pay-as-you-go APIs that users can terminate at any time based on observed performance. We show that this flexibility can break standard contracts, opening them up to abuse by either the providers or users. To address this, we extend contract theory to account for unknown exit rules. We formalize the problem as a Principal-Agent game, and show that budget-optimal contracts in this setting are proportional to anytime-valid hypothesis tests based on likelihood-ratio e-values. We give an efficient algorithm for computing anytime contracts and show how betting e-processes can be used to approximate them when the models are unknown. Finally, we evaluate our findings on synthetic data and data from deployed LLMs.


### [294] [Proportionality in Ranking Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23193)
*Daniel Halpern, Xinyu Liu, Evi Micha, Yu Peng Ng, Warut Suksompong*

Main category: cs.GT

Abstract: Motivated by applications in reinforcement learning from human feedback, we consider a setting where a given collection of rankings needs to be compressed into a smaller collection of rankings. This can also be seen as a combination of two fundamental social choice frameworks: ranking aggregation and multiwinner voting. We propose three proportionality notions that capture different types of representation in this setting: positional proportionality, pairwise proportionality, and proportionality for solid coalitions (PSC). On the one hand, we show that positional proportionality and PSC are always satisfiable for any input rankings and target compression size, and a desired output can be found in polynomial time. On the other hand, we prove that pairwise proportionality cannot be satisfied in general, but can nevertheless be attained when the input rankings are single-peaked or single-crossing.


<div id='q-fin.ST'></div>

# q-fin.ST [[Back]](#toc)

### [295] [Reading the News Without Hindsight: NoLBERT Topics and the Cross-Section of Stock Returns](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22745)
*Kevin Foley, Jonathan Hartadi, Shivesh Prakash, Charishma Takkallapalli, Swapnil Vatsal*

Main category: q-fin.ST

Abstract: Language models that read financial news are usually trained on text from the same years used to test them, so a backtest can reward hindsight. We build news topics from NoLBERT, an encoder pretrained only on text published between 1976 and 1995, adapted into a sentence encoder by contrastive fine-tuning on passages of the same article, with no labels or returns. We compare it with LDA, MiniLM and FinBERT readers on 1.26 million articles from 2012 to 2024, convert each reader's topics into attention shocks and stock exposures, and forecast the returns of the 1,000 largest US stocks with eight models at three rebalancing frequencies. Models are fitted on 2013-2019, choices are made on 2020-2021, and 2022-2024 is held out. The experiment with the best validation score loses money at every frequency. Measuring attention shocks against the past 63 trading days instead of 5 raises the test rank IC of every reader. NoLBERT gives the most stable topics and forecasts about as well as LDA. An equal-weight combination of all readers and models earns a monthly net Sharpe ratio of 1.12, significant against zero before adjusting for the search but not against a price-only benchmark.


<div id='cs.CR'></div>

# cs.CR [[Back]](#toc)

### [296] [Recoverability-Controlled Safety Miscalibration in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22425)
*Yuwei Wang, Xingwang Liang, Yu Xie, Haotian Chi, Haijun Geng*

Main category: cs.CR

Abstract: Safety-aligned language models are expected to refuse harmful requests while remaining useful for benign ones. However, because many evaluations score only the final response, they often conflate intent understanding with response-level safety behavior. To address this, we introduce RiskCueBench, a recoverability-controlled benchmark that separates these two stages by first measuring whether a model can successfully recover a high-level intent and then evaluating its safety behavior on the same prompt. RiskCueBench contains 160 latent intents and 640 abstract non-operational surface prompts across eight risk categories. The benchmark covers 25 completed model groups and 32,000 successful model outputs with final rubric-adjudicated labels. When conditioning on pairs where both surface forms are recoverable, we find that increased contextualized specificity induces a shift toward less safe behavior. Specifically, paired bootstrap estimates show that the refusal rate and global safety score decrease by 0.175 and 0.440, respectively. Furthermore, mixed effects modeling corroborates a 0.107 increase in unsafe cooperation. These results show that the safety behavior can be miscalibrated to the surface form even after the intent has been recovered, motivating safety evaluations that report response behavior conditional on semantic recoverability.


### [297] [The LLM-Attacker: A Unified Red-Team Framework for Privacy Evaluation of Split and Distributed LLM Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22722)
*Georgios Politis, Evangelos Pappas*

Main category: cs.CR

Abstract: Split language models keep input text on a client but send intermediate representations to a server. Attacks can reconstruct text from these messages. Comparing privacy evaluations is difficult when attackers see different messages, know different model weights or use different datasets and recovery measures. We present LLM-Attacker, a framework that records these choices and lets researchers check, compare and extend an evaluation. Compatible attacks reuse recorded messages, and their predictions are saved for independent scoring. The scoring rule requires a linked undefended reference before a defence can pass. Reports also identify attacks that remain untested. In a controlled GPT-2 experiment, adding gradients returned during training increased token recovery from 94.20% to 97.38% and exact-document recovery from 13.71% to 37.77%. The 95% interval for the token gain lay above the two-percentage-point threshold fixed before testing. This experiment is shared with a companion study; here, it tests how the evaluation responds to a change in attacker access. Further studies measure how corpus choice and compression defences affect recovery, compare an adapted published attack with its reference implementation, and test the measurement code. The framework reports implementation checks separately from measured attack effectiveness. Each privacy claim should state the attacker, dataset and scoring rule, report its controls and identify untested attacks.


### [298] [On the (In)stability of Adaptive Fee Mechanisms in Multi-Chain Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23305)
*Yifan Zhou, Jiang Xiao, Xuechao Wang, Ru Cheng, Shijie Zhang*

Main category: cs.CR

Abstract: EIP-1559 has become the dominant transaction fee mechanism in permissionless blockchains, regulating scarce block space through an adaptive base fee. The design balances a trade-off between responsiveness to demand fluctuations and stability of long-term resource usage. On a single chain, this trade-off is well understood and empirically stable. In multi-chain systems, however, cross-chain flows introduce delayed economic interactions across otherwise independent fee markets. These interactions alter the control dynamics and create stability risks that existing models do not capture. Deployed systems have already polarized. Polkadot enforces conservative update rates, effectively disabling fee adaptivity and reverting the market to inefficient first-price auctions. Cosmos adopts aggressive update rates without formal guarantees. To fill this gap, we propose a control-theoretic framework that formalizes multi-chain fee markets as a nonlinear dynamical system with delayed feedback. We identify delay-amplified over-correction as the structural root of instability. Experiments show that cross-chain coupling erodes stability relative to monolithic blockchains, reducing the safe parameter space for base fee update rates by up to 84.8%. Moreover, instability propagates across chains, contaminating the otherwise stable peer chains. At Ethereum's update rate, which leaves a monolithic chain stable, the multi-chain system exhibits extreme load oscillation between full and near-empty blocks, with over half of all blocks at full capacity. Mainstream Cosmos chains already exceed their safe bounds under their default configurations, and the resulting load oscillation raises their block failure rate by an order of magnitude. Guided by this mechanism, we design a lightweight protocol-level mitigation that removes the over-correction and stabilizes dynamics in multi-chain environments.


### [299] [StratFL: Countering Covert Model Poisoning Attacks with Strategic Federated Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22626)
*Manoj Twarakavi, Venkata Sriram Siddhardh Nadendla*

Main category: cs.CR

Abstract: Advanced persistent threats (APTs) are a major concern in federated learning (FL) deployments in critical sectors such as healthcare, and finance. Traditional defense mechanisms fail to contain APTs due to their intermittent attacking behavior. This paper addresses a specific type of APT called covert model poisoning (CMP) where the attacker intermittently injects poisoned model updates in a covert manner. The interaction between the FL server and a CMP attacker is modeled as an imperfect repeated game where both players cannot observe each other's actions. A novel adversarial search algorithm called StratFL is designed alongside novel belief update algorithms at both the server and adversary. Experiments are designed using MNIST and CIFAR-10 datasets to evaluate and compare StratFL's performance against state-of-the-art methods. Empirical convergence of belief update algorithms is demonstrated across various security vignettes. At the same time, StratFL is found to exhibit an improved accuracy of 10% compared to state-of-the-art methods.


### [300] [MobileInject: Evaluating Reasoning-to-Action Security of MLLM-Based Mobile Agents under Cross-Modal Consistency Injection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23307)
*Songping Wang, Peiyang Li, Yi Huang, Fengting Li, Xuhao Ran, Yueming Lyu, Chao Feng, Caifeng Shan*

Main category: cs.CR

Abstract: Multimodal large language model (MLLM)-based mobile agents are rapidly evolving from conversational assistants into autonomous systems that perceive, reason over, and act on smartphone environments. This capability also exposes a new security risk: adversaries can embed malicious objectives in cross-application mobile content and steer agent behavior through indirect multimodal injection. Although prompt injection has been studied in desktop and web settings, its cross-modal properties in mobile ecosystems remain poorly understood, and existing security analyses are still limited. To close this gap, we introduce MobileInject, the first benchmark for evaluating mobile agents against multimodal injection attacks and reasoning-to-action security risks. MobileInject provides a high-fidelity interactive mobile evaluation framework covering 11 scenarios, 9 security-risk categories, and 108 executable risk instances. It further introduces Cross-Modal Consistency Injection to exploit corroboration across modalities, two enhanced attack methods, Multi-Role Collaborative Deception and Reasoning-Camouflage Injection, to intensify trust manipulation and reasoning hijacking, and a Reasoning Security Diagnosis Framework for fine-grained failure analysis. Systematic experiments show that mobile agents are broadly vulnerable to multimodal injection, and that many failures stem from reasoning hijacking at early stages. These findings establish MobileInject as a systematic cross-modal benchmark for studying and improving the security of mobile agents. Code release details are provided in the Appendix Open Science section.


### [301] [From Billion-Scale to Million-Scale Language Models: Operational Capabilities for Embedded Post-Exploitation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23534)
*Gabriel Hortea, Simon Pietro Romano, Juan Tapiador, Mario Varlese*

Main category: cs.CR

Abstract: Small Language Models (SLMs) are increasingly attractive for resource-constrained environments due to their low computational footprint. These characteristics also make them candidates for future autonomous malware, where connectivity, latency, privacy, or operational constraints may preclude cloud-based inference. Yet the ability of ultra-compact models to support malware during post-exploitation remains poorly understood. We systematically evaluate seven open-source SLMs (20M-1.5B parameters) on two post-exploitation code-generation tasks: Directory Enumeration and Backdoor Generation. We compare Python (high-resource) and Lua (low-resource) across prompting strategies, task formulations, iterative reasoning, grammar-constrained decoding, and execution-feedback loops. Results show that offensive code-generation capabilities do not increase linearly with model size. Successful execution depends on the interaction of model capacity, task formulation, and prompting strategy, with task formulation exerting a greater influence than prompt engineering alone. Algorithmic formulation combined with few-shot prompting enables Qwen2.5-Coder 1.5B to reach Pass@5 of 100% in both Python and Lua for Directory Enumeration, 100% in Python for Backdoor Generation, and 99.2% in Lua. Although the evaluated models remain too computationally demanding for widespread deployment in contemporary malware, rapid progress in compact models may reduce this barrier. These findings help anticipate AI-assisted malware and inform future countermeasures.


### [302] [TUPLE-Bench: Evaluating and Mitigating Privacy-Utility Trade-offs in Typed Decision Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23668)
*Yiqu Yang, Qiaoyuan Zheng, Yang Tian, Yu Fan, Elliott Ash*

Main category: cs.CR

Abstract: Typed decision models often return more than a selected category. They also expose probabilities for each candidate and, sometimes, a separate confidence score. These fields support downstream decisions but may reveal information beyond a recipient's authorization. We introduce TUPLE-Bench (Typed decision models' Utility and Privacy Leakage Evaluation Benchmark), a benchmark of paired synthetic cases in which recipients may learn a coarse operational category but not its fine-grained cause. Across nine models, instructions to withhold the cause fail to protect it: 91.88%-99.93% of responses reveal the cause through direct selection or residual scores. Even when causes are excluded from the candidate set, full outputs enable 62.85%-75.35% recovery on held-out cases, compared with 51.04%-57.64% using only the selected category (50% chance). Full outputs also reduce mean downstream cost in all 27 model-task comparisons. However, confidence fields increase leakage without consistently reducing cost. We further introduce workflow-constrained privacy quantization (WCPQ), which reduces secret recovery while preserving the behavior and cost of specified downstream workflows. Our findings highlight the need to audit decision interfaces field by field and workflow by workflow.


### [303] [HardEval: An Execution-Verified Benchmark for AI-Driven Security Hardening and Compliance Remediation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23685)
*Manohar Vellala, Soumya Banerjee, Sandip Roy, Ross Gore, Sachin Shetty*

Main category: cs.CR

Abstract: Implementing Security Technical Implementation Guide (STIG) controls remains a demanding operating-system administration task. Each natural-language security requirement must be translated into a precise and persistent configuration change. These changes span Secure Shell (SSH) configuration, authentication policy, audit rules, and kernel parameters. Existing benchmarks do not measure whether a model can perform this task on a live system. Code benchmarks evaluate isolated programs without persistent system state, while agent benchmarks largely focus on applications rather than operating-system configuration. In this paper, we introduce \textbf{HardEval}, an execution-verified benchmark for evaluating language-model-assisted security hardening. HardEval is built from Red Hat Enterprise Linux 8 STIG controls that fail on an unhardened baseline system. Expert-written reference remediations and Open Vulnerability and Assessment Language (OVAL) checks serve as hidden ground truth for grading; they are never shown to the model, and each task states only the required security outcome while withholding the implementation. The model produces a Bash script, which is executed on a live virtual machine and graded from fail to pass using the corresponding OpenSCAP/OVAL compliance check. Evaluation of fourteen frontier and open-weight configurations reveals large and uneven capability differences across security domains. A recurring failure mode is plausible security code that does not satisfy the exact compliance condition. For four open-weight configurations, multi-run Wilson confidence intervals preserve the observed ordering. HardEval is publicly available.


<div id='cs.SD'></div>

# cs.SD [[Back]](#toc)

### [304] [From Individual Songs to Music Corpora: Evaluating Explainable Harmonic Decision-Making on HookTheory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22416)
*Josef Pavlíček, Martin Pelikán*

Main category: cs.SD

Abstract: Automatic melody harmonization is often evaluated through individual compositions or data-driven corpus models. This study examines whether an explainable harmonic decision model developed for individual songs can be applied to independently collected human annotations without changing its rules. We evaluate a frozen Explainable Melody Harmonizer (EMH) on selected HookTheory excerpts containing aligned melody and chord annotations. EMH generates chord sequences from melody alone using explicit criteria for melodic compatibility, harmonic function, persistence, cadence, meter-aware segmentation, and Viterbi-like optimization. Human chords are withheld during generation and used only for post-hoc comparison. Nineteen excerpts yielded 439 evaluable harmonic segments. Exact chord agreement reached 20.50%, chord-root agreement 31.66%, and harmonic-function agreement 47.62%, with functions assessed relative to the key inferred by EMH. Decision-level analysis identified 203 segments (46.24%) where the human chord lay outside the model's candidate vocabulary and 146 (33.26%) where it was available but not selected. These findings distinguish representational limitations from differences in chord selection without treating the human reference as the only musically valid solution. The study demonstrates a reproducible, interpretable approach to corpus-based evaluation of harmonic decision-making.


### [305] [Framing Spatial Hearing within the Free Energy Principle: A Hierarchical Model of Perceptual Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23196)
*Roberto Barumerli, Nils Meyer-Kahlen, Lorenzo Picinali*

Main category: cs.SD

Abstract: Spatial hearing relies on dynamic internal representations to decode spatial information from ever-changing acoustic conditions. A fundamental example is sound localisation, where listeners infer the direction of an invisible sound. Bayesian inference provides a computational account of such a localisation process; however, modelling efforts are largely limited to static listening scenarios. Instead, the free energy principle (FEP) describes perception, learning, and action as joint minimisation of variational free energy (VFE), providing a principled framework to account for dynamic conditions. Here, we take a first step toward applying the FEP to spatial hearing. We characterise how listeners' beliefs evolve across trials, in which they estimated sound direction and received subsequent visual feedback on each trial. Listeners are described by a hierarchical generative model where visual feedback drives gradient descent on VFE, updating beliefs over three independent quantities: lateral and polar localisation precisions and hemifield disambiguation. The model captures individual learning trajectories and reveals components with different dynamics: lateral precision improves, as does hemifield disambiguation, consistent with increased sensitivity of the auditory pathway in decoding vertical direction. Notably, polar precision also increases, most for listeners with the lowest initial precision, suggesting that the observed vertical improvement is driven by both hemifield disambiguation and polar precision. This work establishes spatial hearing as a tractable domain for FEP and motivates future closed-loop experiments with full action-perception cycles.


### [306] [Interpreting SSL Representations for Spoof Detection: a WavLM Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23526)
*Mohamed Mallat, Michele Panariello, Massimiliano Todisco, Nicholas Evans, Anthony Larcher*

Main category: cs.SD

Abstract: Self-supervised speech models such as WavLM achieve strong spoofing detection performance, yet which layers encode the most spoof-relevant information and what acoustic properties they capture remain opaque. We present a layer-wise interpretability study of WavLM using the ASVspoof 5 database, combining three complementary analyses: (i) per-layer linear probing with attack-level evaluation across speech synthesis, voice conversion, and adversarial attacks; (ii) a canonical correlation analysis (CCA) linking layer representations to spoofing-relevant acoustic features; (iii) an exhaustive grid search over layer subsets showing that use of five layers outperforms full thirteen-layer pooling. CCA analysis reveals that, while most acoustic features peak in alignment with mid-layers and then decay, voice quality measures jitter, shimmer, and the harmonics-to-noise ratio show the opposite trend, with correlation increasing toward later layers. Embedding visualisations reveal that deeper layers capture a continuous acoustic similarity among attacks rather than discrete categories, suggesting these layers encode fundamental synthesis artifacts rather than attack-specific signatures.


<div id='cs.SI'></div>

# cs.SI [[Back]](#toc)

### [307] [Political Narratives in the 2024 Moldovan Presidential Election: Stance, Amplification, and Influence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22464)
*Andrea Piras, Osman Rahmoun, Elena Zheleva*

Main category: cs.SI

Abstract: Social media platforms play a central role in shaping political discourse during elections, particularly in geopolitically contested regions. We study how political narratives emerged, aligned, and propagated during the 2024 Moldovan presidential election by analyzing publicly available posts collected from X, Facebook, and Reddit between August and October 2024. Combining multilingual stance detection, coordination analysis, and causal inference, we examine both descriptive and causal mechanisms underlying online political discourse. First, we apply large language models to classify stance toward European integration, Russia, and the two main presidential candidates, revealing clear cross-platform and cross-linguistic differences in how political positions are expressed. Second, we construct retweet-based and semantic alignment networks on X to identify amplification and aligned framing of dominating narratives. Finally, we use a proximal causal inference framework to estimate peer influence effects in stance formation. Our results indicate consistently positive and statistically significant average treatment effects across major stance categories. These findings provide evidence that amplification and peer exposure contribute to the reinforcement of political narratives online. This work offers the first analysis of social media discourse surrounding the 2024 Moldovan presidential election and demonstrates the value of integrating language-based annotation, network analysis, and causal inference for studying political influence in multilingual and geopolitically sensitive contexts.


### [308] [Validating LLM-generated political networks with survey-reported partisan exposure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23199)
*Marco Tonin, Michele Tizzoni, Bruno Lepri, Luca Maria Aiello*

Main category: cs.SI

Abstract: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used to simulate human behavior in social science research, including decisions, interactions, and social relationships. However, in network formation tasks, existing evaluations often focus on aggregate network properties, while networks with plausible structural properties may still fail to reproduce individuals' social and political environments. Here, we generate LLM-based political networks using personas sampled from the nationally representative ANES 2020--2022 Social Media Study and use respondents' survey-reported partisan exposure as an empirical benchmark for the political environments generated for the corresponding personas. We first characterize the structural properties and political assortativity of the generated networks, and then compare ego-level partisan exposure with the survey. We find that the structure and political assortativity of the generated networks vary across experimental configurations. More importantly, when comparing generated partisan exposure with the survey, some models generate stronger separation between Democratic and Republican egos than reported in the survey, with higher same-party and lower cross-party exposure, while others mainly underestimate Republican exposure across ego groups. Moreover, networks with similar political assortativity can still generate different partisan environments around the same personas, including among networks with plausible structural properties. Finally, generated networks show group-specific errors, under- or overestimating exposure to either Democrats or Republicans for specific social groups. These findings show that evaluating LLM-based social simulations only through aggregate outcomes can hide errors in the local environments through which those outcomes emerge, highlighting the importance of validation at both aggregate and individual levels.


### [309] [Graphlet-based Decentralized Finance Transaction Method Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23466)
*Célestin Coquidé, Alessio Brini, Natkamon Tovanich*

Main category: cs.SI

Abstract: Decentralized Finance (DeFi) transactions involve complex interactions among accounts, smart contracts, and tokens. We investigate whether network topology can identify transaction methods that describe DeFi operations. We extract directed graphlet features to classify the 30 most frequent methods. Our results show that graphlet features complement event log counts in improving classification performance. Using SHapley Additive exPlanations (SHAP), we further derive model explanations that reveal method-specific graphlet structural patterns.


<div id='cs.PF'></div>

# cs.PF [[Back]](#toc)

### [310] [The Optimal Ranking Tradeoffs Between Two Ranking Scores in Two-Class Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23294)
*Sébastien Piérard, Adrien Deliège, Marc Van Droogenbroeck*

Main category: cs.PF

Abstract: The accuracy, true negative rate (negative class recall), true positive rate (positive class recall), negative predictive value (negative class precision), positive predictive value (positive class precision), class-specific intersections over unions, class-specific F-scores, Cohen's kappa, and balanced accuracy are scores that one intuitively wants to maximize when developing machine learning methods. However, they lead to different rankings of such methods. So, how can we establish a ranking that is an optimal tradeoff between any pair of these scores? It turns out that all these scores are special cases of ranking scores: a continuum of scores parameterized by the relative importance values assigned to the true negative, false positive, false negative, and true positive outcomes. Since all these scores are known to induce meaningful rankings, we seek the optimal tradeoffs among them. We consider the tradeoff between two scores to be optimal when it minimizes the Fréchet variance regarding the Kendall distance (also known as the bubble-sort distance) between rankings. For any given set of classifiers to rank, the optimal tradeoff lies at the center of the shortest (geodesic) path on the manifold of all rankings induced by the ranking scores. However, in the parameter space of the ranking scores, it is rarely achieved by averaging the importance values corresponding to the initial scores. In this paper, we present an exact analytical solution for determining a ranking score that is an optimal tradeoff for any given pair of initial scores, in the common case where the class priors are the same for all compared performances. We also experimentally investigate what happens when performance follows some distributions with fixed priors.


### [311] [Data-Driven Prediction of Optimal MPI Rank Placement and Node Count for Memory-Bound CFD on AMD EPYC Clusters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23383)
*Marcin Lawenda, Łukasz Szustak, Aleksandra Krasicka*

Main category: cs.PF

Abstract: Users who run a memory-bound OpenFOAM case on an AMD EPYC cluster must decide how many nodes to use and how many MPI ranks to place on each core complex. The fastest choice is often not the cheapest one. This paper revisits our earlier analytical model of this performance and energy trade-off with 560 measured configurations of the motorBike case on five Zen 4 and Zen 5 EPYC processors, including socket power from processor telemetry. On up to four nodes, small meshes are both fastest and cheapest on a single node on most processors. For the largest mesh the largest node count is both the fastest and the cheapest option, because its speedup is superlinear. A genuine trade-off arises only for medium meshes, where full speed costs a median of 20 to 97% more node hours than the cheapest option. The L3 miss rate and the halo traffic per cell are consistent with this pattern. For a new mesh of the same case, the configuration that was optimal on the nearest measured mesh is near optimal in 75 to 85% of the decisions. Running the two configurations it recommends for speed and for cost raises this share to 85 to 90% for runtime and node hours and determines the trade-off with median errors of 3 to 5 percentage points. Neither gradient boosted trees trained on the same data nor the earlier polynomial model improves on this simple procedure.


<div id='cs.RO'></div>

# cs.RO [[Back]](#toc)

### [312] [DriveWorld3D Evidence Constrained 3D World Modeling and Generative Simulation for Real-World Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22450)
*Wei Wang, Yiru Veronika Wang, Yihan Wang, Zihao Li, Xiaohui Liang*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Three dimensional modeling for autonomous driving must continuously estimate road geometry, obstacle occupancy, traffic participant behavior, and observational uncertainty under limited computational resources. Existing reconstruction methods can synthesize realistic novel views, but visual fidelity does not sufficiently characterize the geometric reliability required for driving. Generative world models can expand scenarios and predict future observations, yet may introduce content unsupported by measurements. We propose DriveWorld3D, a framework combining temporal video learning, measurement constraints from multiple sensors, and offline generative simulation. Its online component maintains a unified world state comprising road surfaces, volumetric occupancy, dynamic entities, and road relations, while separately representing physical occupancy, observability, and evidence provenance. Action conditioned future prediction and risk sensitive planning connect this representation to driving decisions. Its offline component reconstructs recorded scenes using neural radiance fields or 3D Gaussian Splatting, then expands long tail conditions using generative models while restricting the use of generated content as geometric ground truth. We specify data partitioning, sensor ablations, fault injection, paired closed loop testing, and real to simulation consistency assessment. The central hypothesis is that explicit evidence management can reduce dangerous false free space predictions and occlusion risk within a matched computational budget while retaining acceptable driving efficiency. All performance claims remain subject to experimental validation. Keywords: autonomous driving; 3D occupancy; sensor fusion; uncertainty; generative world models; NeRF; 3D Gaussian Splatting; closed loop simulation


### [313] [Sample-Efficient Reinforcement Learning for Policy Improvement via Flow-Reversed Action Anchors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22852)
*Carl Qi, Weijun Dong, Xiaojie Wang, Peiyi Chen, Rahul Sajnani, Mingyuan Zhang, Amy Zhang, Jose Barreiros, Manikatan Nambi, Lillian J. Ratliff, Dylan Randle*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Generalist robot policies offer strong behavioral priors, but achieving deployment-level reliability and throughput remains difficult. Reinforcement learning (RL) can improve these policies, yet its effectiveness is constrained by the cost of online interaction. Existing methods adapt frozen flow-matching policies by treating noise space as a continuous action space. We show that this behaviorally unstructured space makes exploration inefficient, often preventing these methods from extracting meaningful learning signals on challenging tasks. We introduce AnchorQ, which enables coarse-to-fine policy improvement through a structured, refinable latent action representation. At each decision step, AnchorQ uses flow reversal to map reference behaviors to state-conditioned latent anchors, samples local particles around those anchors, and applies value-guided sampling to explore promising refinements. Our analysis shows that anchors reduce identification complexity at the cost of support bias; particle refinement trades this bias against action-class complexity; and critic accuracy determines the reliability of KL-regularized, value-guided selection. Across simulated and real-world manipulation tasks, AnchorQ outperforms state-of-the-art post-training baselines in sample efficiency, delivering a 37% improvement in real-world success rate and 97% higher simulated task throughput under the same interaction budget. Additional visualizations are available on our website this https URL .


### [314] [Unifying Embodied Foundation Models via a Model-to-Policy Formulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23053)
*Xinyi Hong, Mingyang Ren*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Vision-language-action (VLA) models and world-action models (WAMs) have emerged as promising approaches to robot learning and control. These models integrate multimodal perception and action generation, but differ in their architectures, output representations, and learning objectives. In this paper, we present a unified, architecture-agnostic probabilistic formulation for embodied foundation models based on stochastic kernels. Our formulation has two key components: (i) we characterize embodied foundation models through conditional output distributions and action-realization kernels, which together induce task-conditioned robot policies; and (ii) we connect pretraining and post-training to policy deployment, accommodating model-specific pretraining objectives and offline or online post-training strategies. We instantiate the formulation for OpenVLA and DreamZero, covering autoregressive action-token prediction and joint video-action generation.


### [315] [AWAM: Active Trajectory-Conditioned View Imagination for Robust Robot Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23396)
*Jiawei Geng, Zhenyang Liu, Yuzheng Wu, Yanwei Fu*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Rapid progress in embodied intelligence has enabled increasingly capable robots to follow language instructions and manipulate diverse objects. However, strong results in structured environments do not automatically transfer to open-world conditions, where clutter, occlusion, and changing viewpoints can obscure the spatial relationships required for action. To address this perceptual gap, a virtual active-perception paradigm is introduced. AWAM, an Active World-Action Model actively plans where to observe and imagines what will be seen along the planned trajectory before acting. Task-related virtual camera motion is predicted and used as a structured query into a geometry-conditioned world model, producing a reusable spatial prior for manipulation, which is later compressed into a fixed memory and fused with live observations through cross-attention for diffusion-based action prediction. AWAM achieves competitive success rates with 75.9% on LIBERO-Occ, 62.4% on LIBERO-Plus, 76.5% on RLBench-OG Occlusion1, and 94.4% in real-robot trials. These results support improved robustness to the evaluated occlusions and distribution shifts, addressing part of the gap between structured manipulation and less controlled environments.


### [316] [Reason-guided Human Reward Modeling for Preference-based Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23550)
*Anshul Nayak, Shahil Shaik, Jonathan Smereka, Yue Wang*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Reinforcement learning (RL) policies optimized for task rewards may produce behaviors that are efficient but misaligned with human expectations, safety preferences, or intent. This paper presents a reason-guided preference-based reinforcement learning (PbRL) framework that learns a human reward model from trajectory-level feedback. In addition to preference labels, the framework incorporates reason tags explaining why a behavior is preferred, enabling the learned reward to capture both human choices and their underlying rationale. Compared with preference-only reward learning, the reason-guided formulation provides additional supervision for learning human-aligned rewards and improves the interpretability of the resulting policy. The learned human reward is combined with the environment reward during policy optimization, allowing the agent to improve task performance while producing more interpretable, human- aligned, and preference-sensitive behavior


### [317] [Beyond Action Prediction: Learning Search-Free Planning in Latent Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23615)
*Guanchen Du, Jinhao Du, Yanpeng Sun, Shan Zhang, Na Zhao, Zechao Li*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Latent world models learn action-conditioned state transitions in a compact representation space, providing a predictive model for visual planning and control. However, planning with these models typically requires costly test-time optimization over action sequences. We propose to replace this online search with a goal-conditioned inverse model that directly predicts actions from current and goal observations. A key challenge is that action prediction alone neither ensures that goal representations encode action-relevant information nor that predicted actions are consistent with the learned latent prediction model. We introduce Hindsight Inverse JEPA Planning (HIJP), a search-free framework that addresses both aspects during training. HIJP relabels future observations along offline trajectories as goals, providing action supervision across multiple temporal horizons. By retaining gradients through the goal representations, this supervision directly shapes the visual representation. HIJP further feeds predicted actions into the latent predictor model and aligns the resulting next-state representations with encoded successor observations, enforcing action-transition consistency. At inference time, the inverse dynamics model and latent predictor models are applied alternately to generate multi-step action sequences without online search. Across four visual control tasks, HIJP achieves 86.8% overall success and a 1.9-16.5x planning speedup over search-based planning.


### [318] [Has GPT-6-Astra Achieved Embodied Intelligence?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22471)
*SenseNova, Joint Research Team, Zhongang Cai, Ziang Cao, Tailai Chen, Han Chong Chua, Linjun Dai, Hanming Deng, Hokin Deng, Haiwen Diao, Chenjing Ding, Weichen Fan, Xiao Fu, Zerui Gong, Chenyang Gu, Zexin He, Zhi Hou, Bo Li, Qinyue Li, Quanzhou Li, Xiaowan Li, Zhichao Liao, Dahua Lin, Zilong Lin, Bo Liu, Chunxiao Liu, Pengfei Liu, Tianqi Liu, Ziwei Liu, Chen Change Loy, Lewei Lu, Jiapeng Luo, Yuexiao Ma, Liang Pan, Xingang Pan, Oscar Qian, Ziyi Shang, Yuyao Shao, Dawei Su, Dacheng Tao, Qingyi Tao, Quan Wang, Ruisi Wang, Xiaogang Wang, Ziyi Wang, Beichen Wen, Donghao Wu, Jiaxu Wu, Silei Wu, Xiaoming Wu, Zhonghua Wu, Hongcan Xiao, Weiye Xu, Atsushi Yamashita, Lei Yang, Zhe Yang, Baiqiao Yin, Wanqi Yin, Hanfei Zhao, Tianbo Zhong, Long Zhuo, Jing Zuo*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: GPT-6-Astra demonstrates a level of competence in robotic manipulation and navigation that challenges the conventional paradigm of building specialized models for individual tasks and embodiments. In this report, we ask not only how capable GPT-6-Astra is, but what its capabilities imply for the field. First, we probe the limits of standalone GPT-6-Astra. We conduct a large-scale evaluation across 12 representative benchmarks spanning tabletop manipulation, humanoids, mobile platforms, and dexterous hands. Under a unified protocol that allows in-context learning (ICL) from training-set demonstrations and substantially increases the number of execution steps, GPT-6-Astra achieves unprecedented performance (Fig. 1). Future research may focus on improving 1) the generality of ICL 2) cost and execution time, 3) physical-state verification, 4) recovery from irreversible failures, and 5) long-horizon preservation of progress. Second, we study the agentic harness for embodied control. We identify that close-loop coordination between a high-level "System 2" reasoner (i.e., GPT-6-Astra) and a low-level "System 1" controller (e.g., {\pi}0.5) is costly but effective. Third, we examine GPT-6-Astra on real-world deployment. Our experiments on 8 robot configurations reveal substantial gaps: 1) robot demonstrations required for ICL are expensive to collect, and 2) it is implausible to trade longer execution for higher success rates with physical constraints. Fourth, we envision the next generation of embodied benchmarks. We develop a prototype that 1) derives tasks from real-world needs, 2) provides no in-domain demonstrations, and 3) judges agents on a world clock that keeps running while they deliberate. Taken together, our results suggest that GPT-6-Astra has largely crossed the threshold of embodied competence, but not yet that of practical embodied intelligence.


### [319] [When Plans Change, Remember: Replanning-Guided Visual Memory for Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22556)
*Victoria Smelova, Daniil Kazachkov, Georgiy Kazantsev, Iuliia Tsvetkova, Egor Cherepanov, Nikita Kachaev, Artem Latyshev, Aleksandr I. Panov, Alexey K. Kovalev*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Long-horizon, multi-stage Vision-Language-Action (VLA) policies often require visual information that is no longer present in the current observation. Retaining the complete observation history is computationally expensive and highly redundant, motivating selective visual memory. We introduce RePlanMem-VLA (Replanning-Guided Keyframe Memory for VLA), which turns a policy's own plan revisions into an online memory-write signal. Under receding-horizon execution, consecutive action chunks predict some of the same future actions. RePlanMem-VLA treats unusually large disagreement between these temporally aligned predictions as evidence of a task-relevant event. We first compare 35 disagreement measures across single-task autoregressive decoder-only and Flow-Matching Transformers trained from scratch, then validate the selected signals on pretrained OpenVLA-OFT and $\pi_{0.5}$ policies over MIKASA-Robo-VLA tasks. The resulting detectors achieve ROC-AUC of 0.981-0.997. We then use the selected signals in RePlanMem-VLA to trigger updates to a bounded visual memory, with a robot-masked write filter suppressing redundant frames. In the evaluated memory-retention experiments, RePlanMem-VLA preserves task-relevant observations until the later decision point in 100% of episodes. Real-robot replay data further show that event selection and redundancy suppression extend beyond simulation. These results establish plan revision as a policy-internal signal for controlling what a VLA remembers. Website: this https URL .


### [320] [Embodiment-Classifier Guidance: Transferring Frozen VLAs Across Robot Dynamics without Demonstrations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22576)
*Devin Han, Hershraj Niranjani*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Flow-based policies such as $\pi_0$ are trained on a fixed set of embodiments, and transferring them to new ones is an open problem. To adapt a frozen $\pi_0$ to a new embodiment without additional demonstrations or parameter updates, we train a lightweight embodiment classifier to distinguish successful action chunks on the source body from those on the target body, using a few calibration tasks. On transfer tasks that $\pi_0$ saw on its source body but the classifier was never trained on, we nudge each denoising step of $\pi_0$ with the classifier's gradient, which estimates the direction from the distribution of successful actions on the source body toward that on the target body. On the LIBERO simulation benchmark with four modified controllers, embodiment-classifier guidance raises $\pi_0$'s average success on the target bodies from 72% to 80% across 12 suite-controller pairs, with a 31% relative reduction in failures (11 of 12 gain; 5 significant after Holm correction). In simulation, guidance is data-efficient, needing few target-body rollouts per task, which makes it promising for sim-to-real transfer, where rollouts are costly. Guidance is mixed on weak wrists and harmful on a locked wrist, when the classifier lacks data or favors infeasible motions. Collecting more target-body data with guided $\pi_0$ can fill such gaps: on one weak-wrist arm, guided $\pi_0$ solved a task on which unguided $\pi_0$ had zero successes in the original data, and retraining the classifier on the guided successes raised success from 52.5% to 70.7%.


### [321] [Shape-Aware Neural Time Fields for Rigid-Body Motion and Manipulation Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22762)
*Yanming Zhou, Yuchen Liu, Ahmed H. Qureshi*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Rigid-body motion planning is fundamental to robotic transport and manipulation, where both object position and orientation determine whether a body can move through cluttered environments. This coupling makes planning chal- lenging because translation and rotation can be constrained very differently by surrounding geometry. We propose Shape- Aware Neural Time Fields, a physics-informed neural motion planning framework that learns a reusable travel-time, or cost- to-go, representation for rigid-body motion while explicitly accounting for these distinct geometric restrictions. The learned field captures local motion difficulty in translation and rotation and is paired with a sampling-based planner that balances progress toward the goal with the cost of reaching nearby poses. We evaluate the framework across 2D and 3D rigid- body planning tasks and non-prehensile manipulation through object pushing in diverse environments. Results show substantial improvements in planning success and inference efficiency over physics-informed neural planning baselines, while remaining competitive with classical sampling-based planners at significantly lower planning times. Ablation studies further validate the contributions of both the learned representation and the proposed planning strategy. Finally, closed-loop pushing experiments under execution deviations and external disturbances demonstrate that fast replanning enables reactive and adaptive task execution.


### [322] [NestedVLA: Learning to Consolidate and Generate Skills for Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23271)
*Guodong Du, Wanyu Lin*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: Vision--Language--Action (VLA) models promise generalist robotic agents, yet consolidating skills learned across heterogeneous tasks and environments remains difficult: training data is fragmented across platforms, joint multi-task training suffers from interference, and model merging tends to introduce parameter conflicts or require architectural changes. We instead advocate a paradigm in which skills are first learned independently and then consolidated post hoc. To this end, we propose NestedVLA, which replaces parameter aggregation with parameter generation: a nested hypernetwork, conditioned on the target task, synthesizes task-adaptive action-expert parameters that compose with a frozen base VLA. Task-specific compressed VLM residuals are retained separately. This formulation enables flexible task-conditioned skill reuse while mitigating interference. Across three RL benchmarks (MetaWorld, ManiSkill, CALVIN), NestedVLA outperforms the strongest model-merging baseline by 41 pp on average, matches per-task fine-tuning within 1 pp on ManiSkill, and substantially closes the gap on CALVIN, pointing toward a storage-efficient route to skill consolidation. Code is available at this https URL .


### [323] [Beyond Success Rate: Interventional Evaluation of Memory in Vision-Language-Action Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23372)
*Keyuan Luo, Yi Xie, Wanyun Ling, Yuanzhe Li, Ziyue Li, Zhuoqi Zeng*

Main category: cs.RO

Abstract: When we say that a robot uses memory, does that memory actually play a role? Success-rate ablations alone cannot establish the use of task-relevant facts: they change information and computation together, while aggregate rates can hide offsetting gains and losses. We propose an Interventional Memory Evaluation Protocol (IMEP) for vision-language-action (VLA) systems to address four questions: (1) Does memory provide a net benefit? (2) Under what conditions does memory help? (3) Do changes to memory alter task outcomes? (4) Are these effects specific to task-relevant facts? At the retrieval interface, we compare target-fact substitutions with fact-preserving replacements, minimal numerical perturbations, and exact replicas, while assessing decision correctness separately from motor execution success. We evaluate within-episode, cross-episode, and agentic memory. In cross-episode learned-bank experiments, a one-unit-in-the-last-place (ULP) intervention, including its installation procedure, changes 11.9% of seed-paired outcomes, while changing key facts produces no clear additional effect over fact-preserving replacements. To examine when memory helps, we introduce ChainBench, where correct decisions require historical information unavailable in current observations. The full memory configuration raises task success from 39.1% to 74.3%, matching the oracle-decision condition under shared recorded executions. These results show that correct history can support robot decisions, but establishing whether a memory system realizes this benefit requires fact-specific evidence beyond aggregate success rates.


<div id='cond-mat.dis-nn'></div>

# cond-mat.dis-nn [[Back]](#toc)

### [324] [Sampling the XY model with an attention-based neural network](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23783)
*Piotr Białas, Christian D. Morales, Tomasz Stebel, Dawid Zapolski*

Main category: cond-mat.dis-nn

Abstract: We present a transformer-based variational autoregressive network for sampling lattice systems with continuous degrees of freedom and apply it to the two-dimensional XY model near the Berezinskii-Kosterlitz-Thouless transition. The architecture is combined with physics-informed ingredients: locality-informed attention and approximate probabilities. The resulting sampler significantly outperforms existing neural samplers. We train the model on lattices up to $96\times96$, verify with a mode-dropping estimator that no parts of the configuration space are missed, and reproduce the energy, magnetization and helicity modulus obtained with the traditional Markov Chain Monte Carlo algorithm.


<div id='eess.IV'></div>

# eess.IV [[Back]](#toc)

### [325] [Really Need Recursion? Revisiting One-pass Registration with Multi-level Multi-space Contextual Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22423)
*Yue Lin, Shenghai Wu, Mingyuan Meng*

Main category: eess.IV

Abstract: Deformable image registration is a fundamental challenge in medical image analysis. Recent deep learning-based methods typically adopt either one-pass architectures that complete registration in a single network iteration, or recursive architectures that refine registration through multiple passes. While recursive methods often improve accuracy, they incur substantial computational overhead and slower inference speed. In this paper, we challenge the necessity of recursion for high-performance deformable registration and propose M2CG, a one-pass registration network with Multi-level Multi-space Contextual Guidance. It performs multi-step progressive pyramid registration within a single iteration, guided at each step by contextual cues from three levels (previous, current, and subsequent steps) and four spaces (image space, image feature space, segmentation space, and segmentation feature space). Our M2CG supports both unsupervised (without any labels) and semi-supervised (with segmentation labels for partial training data) registration, and has achieved state-of-the-art performance across two well-benchmarked inter-/intra-patient medical registration tasks with eight public datasets. Our findings suggest that the current reliance on recursion may stem from the absence of sufficient contextual cues that comprehensively characterize the remaining distance to the target alignment.


### [326] [Deep Learning for Forest Plantation Mapping in Godavari Districts of Andhra Pradesh, India](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22415)
*Snehal More, Anuj Karpatne, Valerie A. Thomas, Randolph H. Wynne*

Main category: eess.IV

Abstract: Small-area forest plantations play a vital role in the socioeconomic well-being of farmers in Southeast Asia. Most plantations are established on former agricultural land, often on land less suitable for agriculture. Plantations that are converted from natural forest have adverse impacts on biodiversity. Mapping small-area plantations is thus important to understand the dynamics of forest cover in Southeast Asia and to study the social, economic, and ecological effects of this important land cover and land use change. While the small size of forest plantations makes it difficult to detect them using moderate resolution satellite sensors, the problem is exacerbated by the high degree of mixing between plantations, surrounding vegetation, and other land covers, which often show variegated responses in satellite signals across space and time. In this paper, we study the problem of mapping small-area forest plantations in East and West Godavari districts of Andhra Pradesh, India using deep learning methods. Remotely sensed cloud-free data from the Harmonized Landsat Sentinel-2 S10 product were classified using a pixel-level neural network and training data labeled using a field-based survey in concert with expert aerial photo interpretation. We compare the performance of deep learning methods with a baseline random forest classifier in our study region of 21543 sq. km over a period of 3 years and analyze the differences in the results across land cover classes and seasons.


<div id='eess.SP'></div>

# eess.SP [[Back]](#toc)

### [327] [Property Estimation in Geotechnical Databases Using Labeled Random Finite Sets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.22659)
*Changbeom Shim, Youngho Kim, Craig Butterworth*

Main category: eess.SP

Abstract: The sufficiency of accurate data is a core element in data-centric geotechnics. However, geotechnical datasets are essentially uncertain, whereupon engineers have difficulty with obtaining precise information for making decisions. This challenge is more apparent when the performance of data-driven technologies solely relies on imperfect databases or even when it is sometimes difficult to investigate sites physically. This paper introduces geotechnical property estimation from noisy and incomplete data within the labeled random finite set (LRFS) framework. We leverage the ability of the generalized labeled multi-Bernoulli (GLMB) filter, a fundamental solution for multi-object estimation, to deal with measurement uncertainties from a Bayesian perspective. In particular, this work focuses on the similarity between LRFSs and big indirect data (BID) in geotechnics as those characteristics resemble each other, which enables GLMB filtering to be exploited potentially for data-centric geotechnical engineering. Experiments for numerical study are conducted to evaluate the proposed method using a public clay database.


### [328] [Beyond Latent Personalization: Integrating HRV-Conditioned Personalization via LLM for Interpretable Stress Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23555)
*Wei-Hsiang Ma, Ya-Tse Wu, Hsing-Hang Chou, Chi-Chun Lee*

Main category: eess.SP

Abstract: Stress detection based on heart rate variability (HRV) has received increasing attention. However, existing personalized approaches integrate personal attributes with HRV through latent representations, limiting the interpretability of their interactions. In this work, we propose an LLM-enhanced personalized stress detection framework that integrates HRV features and personal attributes through LLM-based semantic interpretation. The proposed framework achieves a 29.9% relative MCC improvement over the strongest baseline on TILES-2018 while providing human-readable descriptions of individual physiological responses. The results highlight the potential of LLM-based semantic interpretation for achieving both effective and interpretable personalized stress detection.


### [329] [ECG-SDI: Single-lead Electrocardiogram Enables Continuous Sleep Depth Index Profiling and Scalable Digital Biomarkers for Sleep Health](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23727)
*Donglin Xie, Qingshuo Zhao, Songchi Zhou, Haoyu Wang, Jingyu Wang, Yuxi Zhou, Thomas Penzel, Shenda Hong*

Main category: eess.SP

Abstract: Conventional sleep staging provides a coarse-grained representation that obscures within-stage depth variation. We developed ECG-SDI, a single-lead electrocardiography (ECG) framework for continuous sleep-depth profiling and digital biomarker extraction. Using 12,392 overnight polysomnographic recordings, we trained and validated a two-stage deep learning model supervised by conventional sleep-stage labels. Correlations with ordered stages excluding rapid eye movement (REM) sleep were 0.828 in the pooled internal test set and 0.848 in the external Cleveland Family Study; corresponding REM classification AUROCs were 0.954 and 0.977. Overnight trajectories captured within-stage variation. Biomarker-based phenotyping identified a disturbed subtype associated with poorer subjective sleep quality and higher all-cause mortality in the Sleep Heart Health Study (adjusted hazard ratio, 1.20; 95% CI, 1.05-1.38). Among 507 participants with paired recordings before and after continuous positive airway pressure (CPAP), normalized sleep-depth area increased by 6.1% and shallow-sleep burden below 0.3 decreased by 16.0%. Exploratory analyses linked reductions in approximate entropy and depth variability to reduced oxygen desaturation and increased sleep efficiency, respectively (Spearman rho = 0.24 and 0.12); both associations remained positive after covariate adjustment. Mean ECG-SDI within N2 increased by 0.017 (95% CI, 0.004-0.030). A wearable proof-of-concept illustrated the acquisition-to-report workflow. These findings support ECG-based sleep phenotyping and further evaluation of CPAP response monitoring at home.


<div id='cs.AR'></div>

# cs.AR [[Back]](#toc)

### [330] [Agentic AI for Heterogeneous System Design Across Chip, Scale-Up, and Scale-Out Domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23724)
*Murali Emani, Kashyap R Manjusha*

Main category: cs.AR

Abstract: The design space of modern AI/HPC systems has expanded rapidly across compute architectures, including GPUs, dataflow processors, and RISC-V accelerators; memory tech- nologies, including HBM, GDDR, and SRAM; and interconnects such as NVLink, UALink, Infinity Fabric, InfiniBand, Slingshot, and Ultra Ethernet. No single architecture is optimal across LLM training, LLM serving, and AI-coupled HPC workloads, while the resulting combinatorial design space is too large for manual exploration. We present ArchAgent, a hierarchical multi- agent framework in which an LLM-based planner decomposes system design into chip, scale-up, and scale-out subproblems, each handled by specialist agents. A deterministic analytical evaluator closes the loop, ensuring that all performance estimates are derived from analytical models rather than generated by the LLM. On a mixed AI+HPC workload under a 1 MW power budget, ArchAgent discovers heterogeneous designs that achieve a 1.93x weighted geometric mean speedup over a homogeneous GPU baseline, while requiring only 18 evaluations compared with 100 evaluations for hill climbing, which achieves 1.65x speedup.


### [331] [qGAMMA: A Framework for Optimal DNN Mapping on Multi-Precision Accelerators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23222)
*Leandro Fiorin, Luigi Altamura, Cristina Silvano*

Main category: cs.AR

Abstract: This work describes qGAMMA, a flexible framework for exploring and optimizing the mapping of mixed-precision Deep Neural Network (DNN) models onto general-purpose spatial accelerators that support a range of fixed- and floating-point precisions. qGAMMA navigates the design space using a domain-specific genetic algorithm and an analytical performance model based on hardware synthesis results. The framework identifies the optimal mapping and accelerator configuration that minimizes target metrics. Extensive evaluations across DNN workloads show that qGAMMA offers valuable insights for designing mixed-precision applications, enabling better trade-offs between performance, energy efficiency, and resource utilization.


### [332] [Memory Bandwidth, Not FLOPs: Profiling and Accelerating Query-Based Segmentation Decoders on Edge GPUs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22377)
*Oliver Busemann, Torben Töniges, Martin Atzmueller*

Main category: cs.AR

Abstract: Query-based segmentation models achieve strong performance even under real-time inference on workstation GPUs. On edge devices, however, we show their transformer decoders are deeply memory-bandwidth-bound, a bottleneck invisible to standard FLOP analysis that worsens with input resolution and limited DRAM bandwidth. An FP16 roofline analysis with tensor-core GEMMs active and hardware DRAM counters shows that the non-tensor-core aggregates of FastInst and Mask2Former reach only $2.5\%$ to $6\%$ of the CUDA-core ridge yet account for $53\%$ to $70\%$ of decoder time and $61\%$ to $71\%$ of decoder bytes. The largest individual kernels sustain up to $90\%$ of nominal peak DRAM bandwidth, effectively saturating the memory bus. The bottleneck is therefore not primarily low GEMM intensity, but cross-kernel activation traffic. CompactInst addresses this bottleneck using parameter-free spatial pooling at $4{\times}$, which reduces the bytes moved within the transformer decoder, while a two-convolution refinement head recovers spatial detail. Under TensorRT FP16 on Jetson Orin Nano, CompactInst delivers decoder speedups from $2.35{\times}$ to $4.74{\times}$ and model speedups from $1.57{\times}$ to $2.62{\times}$ as input resolution grows, while closely matching accuracy ($-0.4$ AP on COCO val2017, $+0.2$ AP on Cityscapes test). A heavier variant with $2.57{\times}$ more decoder FLOPs still runs its decoder $1.74{\times}$ faster on the most bandwidth-constrained device, confirming at the deployment level that decoder bytes, not FLOPs, govern latency.


<div id='cs.CV'></div>

# cs.CV [[Back]](#toc)

### [333] [Three LiDARs, Twenty-Five Datasets: Cross-Source Generalization in Tree-Species Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22606)
*Jae Joong Lee, Bedrich Benes*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: FOR-species20K is the largest public LiDAR benchmark for tree-species classification, pooling 25 independently collected terrestrial, mobile, and airborne laser-scanning datasets across 33 species. We introduce a multi-resolution, multi-view ConvNeXtV2 model that improves official hidden-test accuracy from 79.5% to 85.0%, ranking first at the time of submission. We then examine what this score measures. Because the hidden test is species-balanced, its overall accuracy is more comparable to a macro metric than to accuracy under the public set's long-tailed distribution. Moreover, the official random split places every source dataset in both training and evaluation. A logistic probe recovers source identity from out-of-fold model probabilities with 69% accuracy, while geometric features identify the source above 90% within two widely shared species; 18 of the 33 species occur in only one source. Under a source-species-cell holdout, evaluated on 5,566 trees from eight eligible species that retain the same LiDAR-type training support in other sources, the unweighted species mean falls from 82% to 36%, and tree-weighted accuracy falls from 95% to 60%. Count-matched retraining and experiments with two alternative backbones indicate that this gap is not explained by reduced training support alone, while an external PureForest experiment shows a smaller but consistent grouped-split effect. These results establish the 85% leaderboard score as strong in-distribution performance, but not as an estimate of source-disjoint generalization, motivating balanced metrics and source-aware evaluation. The code is available at: this https URL


### [334] [STRIDE: Evaluating Spatiotemporal Reasoning in Driving Edge Cases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22795)
*Lieqi Liu, Rui gao, Jia-Chen Gu, Wenbo Hu, Zhaobin Mo, Ahmadreza Moradipari, Nejib Ammar, Wei Wang, Nanyun Peng*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: In autonomous driving, perceiving surrounding objects is not enough: failing to understand how they move and interact over time can lead to dangerous decisions. Evaluating vision-language models (VLMs) therefore requires testing whether they connect past spatial observations to future consequences. Yet existing benchmarks often assess edge cases through basic perception questions or test temporal understanding in simple scenarios, leaving complex spatiotemporal reasoning underexplored. We introduce STRIDE (SpatioTemporal Reasoning In Driving Edge Cases), an object-centric benchmark for long-tail, safety-critical driving scenarios. STRIDE covers six task families: spatial perception, spatial understanding, temporal memory, temporal extrapolation, trajectory prediction, and scene-context awareness. It pairs multiple-choice questions probing individual spatiotemporal judgments with related free-form visual question answering (VQA) requiring detailed scene explanations and justifications of driving decisions. We prioritize scenes with unusual object behaviors and complex traffic interactions, generate reference answers from structured dataset annotations through automated procedures, and use limited language-model assistance and human verification to ensure annotation quality. Across 12 proprietary, open-weight, and driving-specialized models, even the best performer, GPT-6-Astra, achieves only 43.4% average multiple-choice accuracy, versus an approximately 20% random-choice baseline. Further analysis reveals that strong performance in describing driving scenes does not necessarily translate to accurate safety-critical judgments, and that models struggle more with reconstructing object motion than recalling past positions. STRIDE thus exposes spatiotemporal driving limitations that aggregate VQA scores can conceal.


### [335] [HAPACT: A Benchmark For Human-Centric Physical Impact Localization in Movies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22895)
*Youngrae Kim, Yejin Jang, Eunho Kim, Youjin Sung, Kun-Woo Song, Sangpil Kim, Sang Ho Yoon*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Physical reasoning about humans in videos has largely focused on everyday interactions, where contact, pressure, pose, and motion support embodied perception and contact-aware applications. Still, short and forceful properties like impact events remain relatively underexplored, despite their relevance to broader understanding of how abrupt physical events affect the human body. To fill this gap, we introduce human-centric impact localization, a new task that localizes target physical impacts temporally within a video and spatially on the human body. We further present HAPACT, a HumAn-centric imPACT dataset and benchmark built from movie clips, containing over 2.8K video-query pairs with more than 20K fine-grained human-annotated frames. We built HAPACT around viewer-grounded annotation, text-conditioned localization, and supervision at frame-level temporal and SMPL-X-based spatial granularity. To demonstrate the effectiveness of our benchmark, we conduct extensive evaluations of existing methods, employing video moment retrieval models for temporal localization and human-scene contact models for spatial localization. For the spatial task, we propose a text-conditioned baseline by reformulating localization as query-guided body-region selection. Benchmark results show that physical impact localization remains challenging for existing methods, motivating HAPACT as a dedicated benchmark to support physical impact reasoning in videos.


### [336] [COMET: Decoupled Distillation, Routing, and Capacity Control for Task-Agnostic Continual Vision--Language Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22907)
*Seungyoon Woo, Keonhee Park, Gunhee Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Vision-language models (VLMs) such as CLIP retain zero-shot recognition ability, but their open-world use often requires task-agnostic continual learning: tasks arrive sequentially, past data are unavailable, task identity is unknown, and the backbone cannot be retrained. We argue that this setting fails VLMs through three coupled pressures beyond the stability-plasticity tradeoff: (1) modal-consistency drift, (2) representation-space interference, and (3) per-task capacity shortfall. We propose COMET (Collaborative Mixture-of-Expert Transfer) to resolve these three issues respectively as follows. A frozen teacher provides only a feature reference on a shared image-text pool; Tri-Affinity Distillation preserves image-image, text-text, and image-text geometry; and Student-Aware Cross-Expert Distillation aligns new experts with compatible prior experts so routing errors degrade gracefully. We design COMET to keep capacity and inference separate: a contextual bandit keeps or merges full per-task experts using post-training signals, while a reconstruction router selects sparse experts at test time without teacher semantics. On the X-TAIL benchmark, we show that each proposed component improves continual learning performance. COMET shows that continual vision-language adaptation can preserve multimodal geometry, coordinate accumulated experts, and allocate capacity jointly when feature reference, expert shaping, routing, and capacity control are separated by construction.


### [337] [FlashReFocus: Interactive Image Refocusing in Seconds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23297)
*Ching-Heng Cheng, Chia-Ming Lee, Ming-Ching Chang, Xin Li, Yu-Lun Liu, Chih-Chung Hsu*

Main category: cs.CV

Abstract: Single-image refocusing changes the focal plane and depth of field of a photograph after capture. It is inherently interactive: users try many focal points and blur strengths on one image, so each edit should be fast and change only the blur. Recent generative methods, however, rerun large diffusion models for every edit. We observe that the two sub-problems differ in kind: recovering the all-in-focus image is ill-posed and needs a generative prior, whereas rendering blur is largely determined by depth and the focal plane. We therefore propose FlashReFocus, which runs diffusion once per photograph and renders every edit with a lightweight network. To address the ill-posed restoration, a one-step diffusion deblurrer with a decoder refiner recovers fine detail efficiently while balancing fidelity and perceptual quality.
